secondary steel ecosystem, enabling safer, more 
efficient and reliable steelmaking through our 
high‑performance solutions, deep customer 
integration, flexible and scalable capacity and 
an unwavering focus on quality and execution 
discipline. FY2025-26 marked our first year as 
a listed company, we remain committed to 
building on this trust and performance. The 
pages that follow will outline why FY 2025–26 is 
not just another year of growth for Monolithisch 
India, but truly The Big Story in our journey 
ahead.

Presenting the year 
in a snapshot
In FY 2025–26, Monolithisch India delivered 
a standout year, combining robust 
revenue growth, stronger profitability 
and timely capacity expansion. The year 
was defined by sharper market visibility, 
deeper customer relationships and 
meaningful gains in operating efficiency 
across our facilities. With brownfield 
projects commissioned, acquired capacities 
smoothly integrated and demand staying 
resilient across the steel value chain, the 
Company now enters its next phase with 
greater scale, reinforced fundamentals 
and a clear commitment to long-term 
value creation. This year will witness the 
company become one of the largest 
manufacturers of silica ramming mass 
globally by installed capacity.
Engineered for extreme 
heat. Designed for reliable 
furnace performance.
INVESTORS
Revenue and EBITDA growth during the year were 
driven by focus on value added improvements in 
products, and enhanced manufacturing efficiencies 
through completion of brownfield capacity 
additions, integration of acquired operations, 
and increased demand from the steel sector. The 
successful completion of brownfield capacity 
additions, integration of acquired operations and 
rising demand from the steel sector supported 
stronger operating performance during the year. 
With a growing presence across key industrial 
clusters, a sharper focus on value-added, 
performance-critical consumables and enhanced 
manufacturing efficiencies, Monolithisch India 
strengthened its platform for sustainable growth and 
long-term value creation.

===== PAGE 7 =====
At Monolithisch India, The Big Story is written in the performance we enable in every furnace, the reliability 
we deliver in every batch and the discipline we bring to every expansion. 
This continuous endeavour has allowed us to meet rising customer expectations with confidence, as 
India’s steel industry moves through rapid expansion and formalisation. Our disciplined approach to CAPEX 
across the manufacturing lifecycle has enabled us to strengthen process capabilities, optimise operations 
and build capacity in line with evolving customer requirements. By engineering high-performance ramming 
mass grades, optimising heat cycles and expanding our share of business with leading secondary steel 
producers, we have reinforced our position as a trusted, high-retention partner at the heart of the induction 
furnace ecosystem.
Since inception, we have remained focused on building an organisation that is resilient, quality-focused and 
execution-driven, one that supports continuous steelmaking with consistency and confidence.
Our close integration with plant teams, solutions tailored to site-specific conditions and dependable 
logistics from strategically located facilities together support higher heats per lining, reduced downtime and 
improved cost efficiency for our customers. Each interaction, trial and upgrade reflects a shared commitment 
to long-term relationships.
The BIG Story: 
4 | Annual Report 2025-26

===== PAGE 8 =====
FY2025-26 marked our first year as a listed entity, The Big Story is about scaling responsibly by 
anchoring growth in performance chemistry and operating discipline. This positions Monolithisch India 
to support the country’s journey towards higher steel capacity with a stronger, more dependable critical 
consumable that is sustainable in both ambition and execution.
Founded in 2018 to manufacture and supply high-performance consumables, the Company has 
consistently expanded its manufacturing capabilities, growing from 36,000 MTPA in 2018 and targeting 
5,76,000 MTPA in H1 2026-27. This growth reflects a disciplined approach to capital deployment, capacity 
creation and long-term manufacturing capability—building a scalable platform to meet the evolving 
requirements of India’s growing steel industry.

===== PAGE 9 =====
Milestones that shaped our growth
The journey of Monolithisch India is a story of focused execution that reflects a deliberate step towards 
becoming a trusted efficiency partner to India’s secondary steel ecosystem. 
2018-19
Laying the foundation
Monolithisch India Limited was 
incorporated at Purulia, West 
Bengal with a clear vision to 
build a differentiated presence. 
The Company established its 
first manufacturing facility with 
initial installed capacity of 36,000 
MTPA, marking the beginning of 
a journey rooted in scale, quality 
and long-term ambition. 
2019-20
From vision to commercial reality
Following multiple trials and 
stabilisation efforts, the Company 
commenced commercial 
production in September 2019. 
Setting the stage for a calibrated 
ramp-up driven by process 
understanding and market 
development.
2020-21
Strengthening the core
Through focused process optimisation 
and operational improvements, the 
Company enhanced productivity across 
its manufacturing lines. These initiatives 
delivered measurable gains in operating 
efficiency and capacity utilisation, 
demonstrating early operating leverage 
within just the second year of operations.
2021-22
Building confidence, driving capability
As operations stabilised in a new geography, 
the Company crossed a significant milestone. 
Customer feedback loops were institutionalised, 
leading to deeper engagement and product 
refinement. During the year, the R&D function 
intensified efforts towards enhanced and 
sustainable formulations, laying the foundation 
for future product leadership.
2022-23
Product breakthrough and market recognition
A defining year of innovation, marked by the 
successful launch of flagship products SLM 999, 
BG 77 and SGB 777, which elevated performance 
benchmarks in the market. Strong customer 
acceptance drove capacity utilisation to ~95%, 
while portfolio expansion in premix ramming 
mass reinforced the Company’s positioning as a 
trusted, high-performance supplier.

Annual Report 2025-26 | 7

===== PAGE 11 =====
It is with this belief that I present to you the first Annual Report of 
Monolithisch India Limited as a listed company for FY 2025–26, 
appropriately themed “The Big Story.” This year has been about 
more than numbers or milestones; it has been about validating 
our purpose, scaling our capabilities and demonstrating that 
a company born in the heart of India’s steel belts can build an 
enduring, performance-led franchise. For us, The Big Story is not 
a slogan; it is the narrative of how listing-led growth, disciplined 
expansion and deep customer trust are coming together to shape 
the next chapter of Monolithisch positioning us as India’s largest 
ramming mass manufacturer by installed capacity of 5,76,000 
MTPA by H1 2026-27.
Economy and sector overview
FY 2025-26 unfolded against a backdrop of steady, if uneven, 
global growth and continued resilience in the Indian economy. 
While many regions grappled with inflationary pressures, tighter 
financial conditions and geopolitical uncertainties, India remained 
one of the fastest growing major economies, supported by 
strong domestic consumption, sustained public investment 
in infrastructure and a gradual recovery in private capex. The 
government’s continued focus on roads, railways, power, housing 
Chairman’s Note
Dear Shareholders,
and urban development translated into robust demand for steel-
intensive assets, underpinning the performance of core industrial 
sectors.
Within this environment, the Indian steel industry held its 
ground, navigating short-term price volatility while benefiting 
from healthy offtake volumes and continued capacity additions. 
Policy continuity around the National Steel Policy 2017, which 
envisages 300 million tonnes of steel-making capacity and 160 
kilograms per capita consumption by FY 2030–31, remained a 
key long-term anchor for the sector. Importantly, the share of 
secondary steel production through induction furnaces continued 
to rise, reflecting the structural advantages of flexibility, lower 
capital intensity and cost competitiveness. This shift is central to 
Monolithisch’s opportunity landscape.
The broader critical consumable industry, closely linked to 
steel, also evolved during the year. Customers increasingly 
favoured organised, quality-conscious suppliers who can provide 
consistency, technical support and reliable supply at scale. As 
plants sought higher productivity and lower downtime, the role 
of critical consumables like ramming mass became even more 
pronounced. In this macro setting, FY 2025–26 offered both 
In business, as in life, the 
most powerful stories are 
not those we tell about 
ourselves, but those 
that are proved, quietly 
and consistently, by our 
actions.

Monolithisch India Limited

===== PAGE 12 =====
challenges and opportunities—and we are pleased that your 
Company responded with agility, prudence and conviction.
Global and domestic industry context
Globally, the steel and critical consumable landscape is 
undergoing a gradual but decisive transformation. On one hand, 
producers face the twin imperatives of cost competitiveness and 
decarbonisation; on the other, they must manage cyclical swings 
in demand while investing in modern, efficient capacities. This 
is leading to greater emphasis on energy-efficient operations, 
longer campaign lives for furnaces and stricter control over quality 
and safety parameters. Suppliers who can offer performance-
enhancing, reliable solutions are becoming integral partners rather 
than peripheral vendors.
India sits at the heart of this shift. As the world’s second-largest 
producer and consumer of steel, the country continues to add 
capacity across both integrated and secondary routes. The 
induction furnace-based secondary steel segment, where ramming 
mass is a critical consumable, accounts for a meaningful share 
of domestic production and is expected to grow further as new 
capacities come on-stream and older assets are upgraded. 
Within this ecosystem, we see clear trends shaping the future of 
our industry. First, there is a steady shift from unorganised, small-
scale suppliers towards organised players who can offer consistent 
quality, technical advice and dependable supply chains. Second, 
Customers increasingly view ramming mass as a performance 
lever that can influence lining life, furnace stability and operating 
efficiency. Third, as India aspires to 300 million tonnes of capacity, 
the need for partners who can scale responsibly and support 
continuous operations will only intensify.
For Monolithisch India, these trends are not abstract. They are the 
canvas on which The Big Story is being drawn.
Operational review and competitive advantages
FY 2025–26 was a year of significant operational progress for your 
Company. During the year, we successfully executed a major 
brownfield expansion, increasing our installed manufacturing 
capacity from approximately 132,000 MTPA to 250,000 MTPA. 
standalone and consolidated installed capacity reached 307,600 
MTPA following the integration of MIGPL. 
Following the integration of MIGPL, the Company’s effective 
installed capacity stood at 307,600 MTPA. We now operate from 
locations that offer structural freight advantages into major steel 
belts across eastern and central India, enabling competitive 
logistics and timely deliveries even as volumes increase. 
Importantly, capacity was ramped up in a calibrated, demand-
linked manner, with a strong emphasis on stabilising processes, 
maintaining product uniformity and ensuring that expansion 
translated into sustainable, not episodic, growth.
Our operations during the year were anchored in a few core 
differentiators. First, consistent product performance: ramming 
mass is a small cost component but has a disproportionate impact 
on furnace integrity and safety, and our ability to deliver uniform 
quality across large volumes has been a key driver of customer 
trust. 
Second, close technical collaboration: our teams work alongside 
furnace operators and plant engineers to tailor blends to specific 
operating conditions, optimise heat cycles and respond quickly to 
any performance deviations.  
Third, supply reliability: our road-led logistics network, backed 
by capacity and inventory planning, allowed us to maintain high 
delivery reliability even as we undertook capacity expansion.  Steel 
plants operate continuously, making supply reliability and product 
consistency critical to our customers. We strengthened operational 
discipline through sharper monitoring of capacity utilisation, 
rejection rates, on-time delivery and energy intensity—driving 
greater process control, asset productivity and predictability across 
our operations.
Financial review – The numbers behind The Big Story
Even as detailed financials appear in the subsequent sections of 
this Annual Report, I would like to highlight the broad contours of 
our performance.
For FY 2025–26, Monolithisch India reported consolidated 
revenue of `135.29 crore, representing year-on-year growth of 
around 38.98% compared with FY 2024–25. This growth was 
predominantly volume-led, supported by deeper penetration 
within existing customers, improved product mix and incremental 
contributions from the consolidated business. Our strategy of 
increasing our share of business with key accounts, rather than 
chasing purely transactional volumes, has allowed us to grow on a 
stable and diversified base.
EBITDA for the year stood at approximately `31.96 crore , implying 
an increase of about 51.64% over the previous year and reflecting 
operating leverage from higher capacity utilisation, process 
optimisation and automation initiatives. EBITDA margins expanded 
by approximately 197 basis points year-on-year, driven by a 
combination of efficiency gains, better product mix and tight cost 
control, even as we continued to invest in talent and governance 
systems. The launch of SGB limited stands as the Company’s most 
advanced and premium offering, developed through continuous 
research, operator feedback and detailed performance analysis. 
Built on select mineral inputs with refined grain engineering, 
it delivers enhanced lining life, improved furnace stability and 
better thermal efficiency. This translates into stronger operating 
economics, positioning it as a performance-driven solution for 
critical and high-demand applications.
Profit after tax stood at `23.03 crore, compared with `14.36 crore 
in FY 2024–25, translating into PAT growth of roughly 60.35%. Over 
the period from FY 2022–23 to FY 2025–26, PAT has thus grown 
from `4.54 crore to `23.03 crore, underscoring the scalability and 
resilience of our operating model. Return ratios have remained 
healthy, with ROE and ROCE stood at 27.76% and 22.85% 
respectively for the year, the impact of fresh equity infusion and 
ongoing capex.
Our balance sheet is stronger than before. The successful listing on 
the NSE SME platform in June 2025, completed at a premium to 
the issue price, enabled the Company to raise `82.02 crore through 
primary capital. The proceeds are being deployed in line with the 
stated objectives, including capacity expansion, investment in 
subsidiaries and enhancement of manufacturing infrastructure. 
For Monolithisch, the financial narrative of FY 2025–26 is one of 
For Monolithisch India, these trends are 
not abstract. They are the canvas on 
which The Big Story is being drawn.

measured acceleration: strong top-line growth, proportionately

build-out in our history.
Upcoming upgrades and expansion roadmap
If FY 2025–26 was the year of listing-led validation and brownfield 
expansion, the years ahead will be about completing our 
greenfield project and widening our product canvas. 
Further, the greenfield facility is being built as a next-generation 
unit that incorporates learnings from our brownfield expansion. 
We are using this opportunity to optimise plant layout, streamline 
material flow, integrate higher levels of automation and embed 
advanced monitoring systems from day one, thereby reducing 
ramp-up time and ensuring consistent quality at higher 
throughputs. It will be powered by a 2MW solar plant..
Technology integration and infrastructure
A modern business is as much about technology and process 
discipline as it is about raw material and capacity. During FY 2025–
26, we intensified our focus on automation, data-driven operations 
and infrastructure quality. Automation across key stages such as 
crushing, screening, mixing and packing has reduced manual 
intervention, improved consistency and enhanced throughput. 
The adoption of advanced process equipment has allowed us 
to exercise tighter control over grain size distribution, chemical 
dosing and batch uniformity, which in turn translates into more 
predictable performance at the customer’s furnace.
We have strengthened our internal monitoring systems, enabling 
real-time visibility of critical parameters. Monthly reviews now track 
capacity utilisation, rejection rates, customer feedback on lining 
life and on- time dispatch performance, allowing us to respond 
quickly to emerging trends. This culture of measurement and 
accountability supports both operational excellence and strategic 
decision-making.
Our infrastructure enhancements have extended beyond 
machinery. We invested in improving storage and handling of key 
raw materials and enhanced maintenance practices to minimise 
unplanned downtime. On the organisational side, we added 
experienced professionals in plant management, production 
planning, quality control and technical services, ensuring that 
technology investments are matched by the right human capital.
We are in the process of strengthening our digital interface with 
customers through the development of a dedicated customer 
application. The platform provides customers with a consolidated 
view of their purchase orders and products, enabling them to 
place orders digitally, access order and product information, and 
track the status of their orders in real-time. This digital interface 
enhances transparency, convenience and responsiveness while 
enabling more efficient coordination between customers and our 
internal sales and supply-chain teams. As we scale our operations, 
the platform is expected to support a more connected, data-
enabled and customer-centric approach to business.
Taken together, these initiatives are moving Monolithisch India 
steadily up the technology curve—from an efficient producer to 
an engineered solutions partner, and from a capacity-constrained 
business to a scalable, technology-enabled platform. The use of AI-
driven analysis of furnace lining failures will further strengthen our 
ability to identify failure patterns, improve product performance 
and enable more informed, data-led solutions for customers.
Sustainability – Building responsibly, operating efficiently
While we are at an early stage of our formal sustainability 
journey, the principles behind it have long been embedded in 
how we operate. Our approach is pragmatic: to align growth 
with responsible resource use, efficient energy consumption and 
a safe, compliant work environment.
From an energy perspective, process optimisation and 
automation have helped reduce energy intensity per unit 
of output, even as volumes increased. Better layout design, 
improved material handling and preventive maintenance 
practices are enabling us to “produce more with less” - a simple 
but powerful slogan that benefits both our cost structure and 
the environment. We are actively installing renewable energy 
options, including solar power for non-critical loads, as part of 
our medium-term roadmap.
Raw material efficiency is another important pillar. By sourcing 
consistent, high-quality raw materials and optimising our 
formulations, we minimise variability, reduce rejections and 
make better use of every tonne of stone that enters our plants. 
Emission control systems have been installed at key points to

Internally, we continue to strengthen safety standards, compliance 
with applicable environmental and labour regulations, and 
awareness among employees on responsible operating practices. 
As our scale increases, we recognise that our responsibilities to 
communities, regulators, employees and customers will expand as 
well. The Big Story, for us, must therefore be about enduring value, 
not transient growth.
Closing note
FY 2025–26 will be remembered in Monolithisch India’s history 
as the year when our ambitions, capabilities and opportunities 
widened. 
On behalf of the Board, I would like to express my sincere gratitude 
to our shareholders, customers, employees, lenders, suppliers and 
all other stakeholders for their trust and partnership during this 
pivotal year. Your support has enabled us to convert a promising 
story into The Big Story.
We step into the future with humility about the work ahead and 
confidence in the foundation we have built. I invite you to read the 
pages that follow and see, in greater detail, how Monolithisch India 
is engineering its next chapter quietly, consistently and with an

Monolithisch India Limited
Our research focus
	 Our research and application work is centred on one objective: helping customers get more safe, stable heats 
from every furnace campaign. We continuously study the behaviour of different raw material sources, grain 
size distributions and binder systems under induction‑furnace conditions.
	 Plant and technical teams jointly run trials at customer sites, tracking lining life and power consumption to 
understand how small formulation changes translate into operating gains. Insights from these campaigns will 
help us move towards offering complete lining solutions rather than single products.
The Company operates as a leading manufacturer of premixed ramming mass, 
a critical consumable used in induction furnaces across India’s steel and foundry 
ecosystem. With one of the largest premix facilities, Monolithisch delivers 
consistency, scale and operational efficiency. Company is set to become one of the 
largest manufacturers globally by installed capacity in H1 2027.
It serves a wide spectrum of integrated and secondary steel producers, supplying 
solutions that enhance thermal efficiency, improve furnace life and reduce 
downtime. Its strong technical capabilities and product reliability have enabled 
deep customer integration and repeat business across key clusters.
Quality and consistency remain at the core of our customer relationships, enabling 
predictable furnace performance, longer lining life and reduced operational 
disruptions.
A focused approach towards high-performance solutions, combined with capacity 
expansion and process optimisation has strengthened its positioning in a growing 
industry driven by infrastructure and steel demand. 
The Company aims to leverage its expertise in silica processing, developed through 
its experience in the steel sector, to expand into diverse high-value applications. 
Building on this capability, it intends to develop and manufacture high-purity silica 
products for emerging industries in the years ahead.

  ECONOMIC REVIEW
Global Economic Overview
The global economy remained resilient through much of 
2025, but the quality of that resilience became more uneven 
as the year progressed. While activity held up better than 
many feared, the backdrop shifted from one of gradual 
normalization to one of heightened caution because higher 
trade barriers, geopolitical tensions and renewed energy-
market stress began to weigh on confidence and pricing 
expectations.
According to the IMF’s April 2026 World Economic Outlook, 
global growth is projected at 3.1% in 2026, after the world 
economy had already absorbed elevated uncertainty during 
2025. The IMF also noted that global headline inflation 
is expected to rise modestly in 2026 before resuming its 
decline in 2027, highlighting that inflation has not fully 
exited the system and remains sensitive to energy and trade 
disruptions.
From a review standpoint, the most important takeaway 
is that the global economy did not enter FY 2025-26 in 
recessionary phase, but neither did it transition into a clean 
expansion cycle. Instead, the period was characterized by a 
more fragile form of growth in which businesses, lenders and 
consumers operated with shorter planning visibility, making 
purchasing patterns less predictable and causing investment 
decisions to become more selective.
This matters for industrial businesses because such a  macro 
environment like this often does not destroy demand 
outright; rather, it changes the speed and timing of orders. 
End-markets such as manufacturing, construction, capital 
goods and cross-border trade continue to function, but 
they become more sensitive to input-cost shocks, freight 
disruptions, currency movements and delayed project 
approvals.
In practical terms, the global economic review for FY 2025-26 
can be described as one of resilience under strain. The world 
Management 
Discussion and 
Analysis Report
economy showed an ability to continue grow, yet the drivers 
of that growth became narrower and more vulnerable to 
geopolitical headlines, especially where energy and trade 
routes were concerned.
   Outlook
The IMF expects global growth to improve slightly to 3.2% 
in 2027, assuming the current conflict remains limited in 
duration and scope. At the same time, the IMF has warned 
that downside risks dominate the outlook, particularly 
from a longer Middle East conflict, worsening geopolitical 
fragmentation, renewed trade tensions and financial-market 
instability.
The outlook therefore remains constructive, but it is not 
broad-based enough to justify complacency. In the near 
term, the global economy appears more likely to experience 
moderate expansion with periodic volatility rather than a 
strong and synchronized upswing.
A detailed reading of the outlook suggests that energy 
sensitivity is the principal macro variable to watch. If energy 
markets remain contained, the global system can still deliver 
stable, if unspectacular, growth; if energy disruptions deepen, 
inflation and financing pressures could re-emerge quickly 
and compress real demand across multiple regions.
For management purposes, the global outlook supports 
cautious optimism rather than aggressive expansion.. 
Businesses may still plan for continuity in demand, but 
should remain alert to the possibility that policy moves, 
commodity volatility and geopolitical events could alter 
customer behavior with limited warning.

Indian Economic Overview
India remained the fastest-growing major economy in FY 
2025-26, with the World Bank reporting GDP growth of 7.6%, 
up from 7.1% in FY 2024-25. This expansion was supported 
by robust domestic demand, stronger manufacturing 
activity, resilient services and a relatively benign inflation 
environment for much of the year.
Headline inflation averaged 1.9% between April 2025 and 
February 2026, compared with 4.7% in the same period of 
the previous year, largely due to lower food prices. Wholesale 
price inflation averaged 0.4% during April 2025-February 
2026, while the Reserve Bank of India cut the policy rate 
by a cumulative 125 basis points between February and 
December 2025, helping ease monetary conditions.
India’s external and fiscal indicators also remained broadly 
manageable through FY 2025-26. The current account 
deficit narrowed to 1.0% of GDP in the first three quarters of 
FY25-26, while the general government deficit improved to 
7.4% of GDP from 7.7% in FY25, reflecting continued fiscal 
consolidation.
The review of India’s economic performance is notable 
not merely because growth remained high, but because 
the growth composition appeared healthier than a narrow 
export-led surge. Private consumption was supported by 
rising disposable incomes, low inflation for much of the 
period, personal income tax cuts and GST rationalization, 
suggesting that domestic demand remained the principal 
ballast of the economy.
This has strategic importance for industrial sectors because 
domestically anchored growth tends to be more durable 
when the external environment is uncertain. A strong internal 
market allows investment, manufacturing and consumption 
to reinforce each other, thereby reducing vulnerability to 
external demand shocks relative to more export-dependent 
economies.
Overall, the Indian economy in FY 2025-26 displayed both 
scale and balance. Growth accelerated, inflation remained 
largely manageable, fiscal consolidation progressed and 
external balances stayed within tolerable range, creating a 
macroeconomic environment that remained supportive for 
industry.
   Outlook
The World Bank projects India’s growth at 6.6% in FY 2026-
27, reflecting the expected drag from higher global energy 
prices and uncertainty linked to the Middle East conflict. It 
also indicates that inflationary pressures may rise as higher 
oil and gas prices pass through the economy and temper 
part of the consumption support that tax and GST measures 
provided earlier.
The near-term outlook is therefore one of moderation, not 
weakness. India is expected to remain among the fastest-
growing large economies, but the pace of expansion is 
likely to normalize as imported inflation and softer external 
demand reduce some of the exceptional support seen in FY 
2025-26.
A useful way to interpret the outlook is that India’s domestic 
fundamentals remain sound, but the external environment 
has become less forgiving. Private consumption may 
decelerate modestly, government consumption growth 
may soften and exports could feel the impact of shipping 
disruption and slower growth in key trading partners.
Even so, India’s macroeconomic buffers remain stronger than 
many peers, supported by domestic demand depth, policy 
flexibility and ongoing reform momentum. The economic 
outlook remains constructive, although more moderate 
than FY 2025-26, and continued vigilance over inflation, 
fiscal management and the external account will remain 
important.

INDUSTRY STRUCTURE AND DEVELOPMENTS
Global Steel Industry Overview 
The global steel industry is emerging from an extended 
adjustment phase, with demand having effectively bottomed 
out over the 2025–2026 period rather than entering a strong 
upcycle. According to the World Steel Association’s latest 
short‑range outlook, global steel demand reached a trough 
in this window after several years of subdued growth and 
structural rebalancing across major economies.
Worldsteel’s April 2026 communication indicates that global 
steel demand is now expected to increase by only 0.3% in 
2026 to 1,724 million tonnes, rather than the stronger 1.3% 
rebound that had been projected in the October 2025 
outlook. This downward revision reflects the drag from higher 
energy prices, trade disruptions and weaker confidence 
associated with the escalation of conflict in the Middle East.
The demand pattern remains highly differentiated by region. 
Worldsteel continues to see relatively better conditions 
in the developing world (excluding China), where steel 
demand growth is driven by infrastructure investment and 
industrial development in economies such as India and 
selected ASEAN and MENA countries. In contrast, demand in 
advanced economies remains constrained by tight financing 
conditions, elevated costs and soft manufacturing sentiment.
China remains central to the global balance, with worldsteel 
highlighting that the prolonged real‑estate correction 
continues to weigh on steel consumption. At the same time, 
the Association notes that the pace of decline in Chinese 
Financial Statements
Statutory Reports
Company Overview
Annual Report 2025-26 | 17

===== PAGE 21 =====
steel demand is expected to slow, and that by 2027 demand 
there may broadly stabilize as the housing market finds a 
floor. This means China is no longer the undisputed engine 
of incremental steel demand, but it remains the single 
largest influence on the global supply‑demand equation.
Overall, the updated review suggests an industry that has 
absorbed the worst of the post‑2022 adjustment but is still 
operating in a landscape of modest growth and elevated 
risk. For steelmakers and their supply chains, this translates 
into a focus on cost competitiveness, portfolio flexibility and 
regional positioning rather than expectations of a sharp, 
synchronized demand surge.
   Outlook
Worldsteel’s April 2026 short‑range outlook now points 
to a more subdued recovery in 2026, followed by a firmer 
improvement in 2027. The Association expects global steel 
demand to grow by 0.3% in 2026 to 1,724 million tonnes 
and then accelerate by 2.2% in 2027 to around 1,762 million 
tonnes.
The revised outlook confirms the trajectory previously 
identified in the October 2025 SRO: global steel demand has 
broadly bottomed out in the 2025–2026 period and is set 
to move into a gradual recovery phase thereafter. However, 
the pace of that recovery has been tempered by the latest 
conflict‑related energy and trade shocks, leading worldsteel 
to scale back its 2026 expectations.
Regionally, worldsteel anticipates that demand excluding 
China could grow by around 4.0% in 2027, a rate it 
describes as unusually strong for recent years, underpinned 
by infrastructure investment, industrial restocking and 
improved financing conditions in several developing 
economies. In contrast, Chinese steel demand is expected 
to remain broadly flat in 2027 relative to 2026, on the 
assumption that the real‑estate downturn stabilizes but 
does not quickly reverse.
From a strategic perspective, the outlook implies that 
industry growth will be driven disproportionately by 
developing regions and by sectors tied to infrastructure, 
energy and industrial investment. At the same time, 
worldsteel warns that this recovery path remains vulnerable 
to further escalation in the Middle East conflict, sharper trade 
fragmentation or renewed financial stress, any of which 
could again alter demand trajectories.
For management commentary, the updated outlook 
supports a stance of cautious recovery rather than 
exuberance. Volume conditions should gradually improve 
over the next two years, but pricing power, margin stability 
and capacity‑utilization gains are likely to depend heavily on 
regional positioning, cost structures and the ability to serve 
the more resilient end‑use segments.

Indian Steel Industry Review
India’s steel industry remained one of the strongest 
large-scale steel markets in the world during FY 2025-26, 
supported by robust domestic demand, infrastructure 
activity and broad-based industrial consumption. According 
to the Ministry of Steel, crude steel production during April 
2025 to March 2026 stood at 168.4 million tonnes, reflecting 
year-on-year growth of 10.7%.
Finished steel production reached 160.9 million tonnes 
during the same period, up 9.7% over the previous year, 
while finished steel consumption rose to 163.7 million 
tonnes, recording growth of 7.6%. These numbers indicate 
that the industry’s expansion was not driven by production 
alone; domestic absorption also remained strong enough 
to support higher capacity utilization and sustained output 
growth.
India’s trade position improved materially during FY 2025-
26. Finished steel exports rose to 6.60 million tonnes from 
4.86 million tonnes in FY 2024-25, while imports declined 
to 6.52 million tonnes from 9.55 million tonnes, enabling 
18 | Annual Report 2025-26
Monolithisch India Limited

===== PAGE 22 =====
India to become a net exporter of steel for the full year by a 
margin of 0.08 million tonnes.
The review of the year suggests that Indian steel growth was 
supported by a healthier market structure than in many other 
regions. Demand came from infrastructure, construction, 
railways and manufacturing, which helped create a broad 
consumption base rather than dependence on one isolated 
segment. This is important because diversified domestic 
demand usually improves the durability of growth and 
reduces vulnerability to global steel-cycle disruptions.
Another notable aspect of the review period was the 
resilience of Indian steel production in a volatile global 
setting. The Ministry of Steel, citing World Steel Association 
estimates, noted that India’s crude steel production for 
calendar year 2025 reached 164.9 million tonnes, up 10.4% 
over 2024, reinforcing India’s standing as the world’s second-
largest crude steel producer. Even in February 2026, India’s 
crude steel output rose 7.7% year on year to 13.60 million 
tonnes despite softer global conditions.
Overall, FY 2025-26 was a year of strong operating momentum 
for the Indian steel industry. Production, consumption and 
exports all improved meaningfully, while import dependence 
declined, indicating stronger domestic competitiveness and 
healthy underlying demand conditions.
   Outlook
The latest World Steel Association Short Range Outlook of 
April 2026 states that India will remain the world’s fastest-
growing major steel market. worldsteel projects Indian steel 
demand to grow by 7.4% in 2026 and further accelerate to 
9.2% in 2027.
This outlook is especially encouraging because it comes at 
a time when global steel demand growth has been revised 
down sharply. In a weak global market, India continues to 
stand out as the principal growth engine, supported by 
broad-based strength across steel-consuming sectors. 
Worldsteel attributes India’s positive demand trajectory to 
continued growth in infrastructure and the automotive 
sector, along with support from railway network expansion 
and broader economic activity. This means the outlook is not 
merely cyclical; it reflects the structural depth of steel use 
across the Indian economy.
The Indian steel outlook remains firmly positive, though 
margin performance may still depend on raw material 
costs, freight conditions and global price movements. 
Ministry-linked reporting also indicates that coking coal 
prices remained volatile and logistics costs were affected 
by geopolitical tensions, which may continue to influence 
profitability even as demand stays strong.
Even so, the industry appears well positioned for another 
phase of expansion. If infrastructure execution, manufacturing 
growth and domestic investment continue at current levels, 
India is likely to strengthen its role not only as a leading steel 
consumer, but also as one of the most consequential growth 
markets in the global steel landscape.

Favourable government initiatives for the steel and 
allied sectors
The steel ecosystem in India, including associated industries 
such as refractories and ramming mass, continues to benefit 
from a supportive policy environment. Key initiatives during 
the FY 2025–26 period and beyond include:
Green Steel Taxonomy framework: The Ministry 
of Steel operationalised the Green Steel Taxonomy 
to define low-emission steel and create a framework 
for encouraging energy-efficient and non-fossil-fuel-
based steel production.
Green 
steel 
certification 
mechanism: 
The 
government introduced Green Steel Certificates 
for eligible plants, with the National Institute of 
Secondary Steel Technology serving as the nodal 
agency for measurement, reporting, verification and 
certification.
Early industry adoption: By August 2025, 42 iron 
and steel producers had already applied for green 
steel certificates, indicating that decarbonization 
policy is beginning to move from concept to 
implementation.
Support for sustainable market development: 
The taxonomy provides a structured base for future 
green procurement, sustainability-linked financing 
and lower-carbon supply-chain positioning in the 
domestic steel ecosystem.
Record mineral block auctions: The Ministry 
of Mines reported that 200 mineral blocks were 
auctioned in FY 2025-26, the highest ever in a single 
year, including 40 iron ore blocks and 76 limestone 
blocks relevant to steel and allied industries.
Improved raw-material security: The broad 
mineral auction pipeline strengthens long-term 
availability of core industrial inputs and supports 
expansion planning in steel, cement and related 
manufacturing sectors.
Critical minerals focus: The successful auction 
of 22 critical mineral blocks reflects a wider policy 
push toward strategic resource security, which is 
increasingly relevant to industrial manufacturing 
and advanced materials supply chains.

Quality Control Order calibration: The Ministry

steelmakers, but also for allied sectors such as

logistics and industrial analytics.

initiatives show that government support is now 
extending beyond output growth toward greener 
steelmaking, better mineral security, improved 
standards, more circular resource use and digital 
transformation.

headroom for multi‑year growth

Global Ramming Mass Industry Overview
The global ramming mass industry is being shaped by the 
expansion of steelmaking, foundries and high-temperature 
metallurgical applications. 1 ton of steel requires 30 kg of 
ramming mass making it a crucial consumable Ramming 
mass is a high-temperature resistant material used primarily 
in the lining of induction furnaces, making it integral to 
the metallurgical industry. The same source estimates the 
global ramming mass market at USD 2.62 billion in 2025 and 
projects it to reach USD 4.74 billion by 2034, implying a CAGR 
of 6.8% over the forecast period. It also notes that Asia-Pacific 
is witnessing robust growth because of rapid industrialization 
and expansion in steel and non-ferrous metal industries.
The review of the global ramming mass industry suggests a 
market that is becoming more strategically relevant within 
furnace economics. Although the segment is smaller than 
core steel or mining markets in absolute size, its importance 
is amplified by the role it plays in lining performance, furnace 
uptime, thermal efficiency and campaign life.
This means the industry should not be seen merely as a raw-
material adjunct. In modern melting operations, product 
consistency, life and operational reliability have direct cost 
implications, which raises the value of reliable ramming mass 
suppliers.
Another notable review point is that the market’s growth 
profile appears tied to industrial utilization rather than 
speculative demand swings. Since the material is consumed 
in actual metallurgical operations, its demand base tends 
to reflect real production activity in steel, foundry and non-
ferrous segments.
The industry narrative is therefore not just about material 
supply, but about furnace efficiency, thermal performance 
and productivity economics. As end users focus more 
on energy efficiency and operating reliability, suppliers 
of consistent-quality ramming mass can occupy a more 
strategic role in the value chain.
   Outlook
The global outlook for ramming mass remains positive 
because the underlying end-use sectors continue to require 
reliable, heat-resistant lining materials. It is expected that 
technological advancement, stronger energy-efficiency 
focus and Asia-Pacific industrial expansion to remain core 
drivers through the forecast period.
It is also estimated that the industry will likely grow through 
capability upgrading as much as through volume. Customers 
increasingly value lining materials that can improve furnace 
productivity, reduce downtime and support more energy-
efficient operations, which shifts competition toward quality 
and application performance.
This suggests a market that should grow steadily rather 
than cyclically in isolation. Its outlook remains closely tied 
to metallurgical throughput, furnace modernization and the 
operating economics of steel and alloy production.
In that sense, the segment offers a relatively favourable 
outlook so long as industrial production and metal 
processing continue to expand in Asia and other emerging 
manufacturing regions. Growth may not be dramatic in 
any single year, but it appears durable and operationally 
anchored.
Indian ramming mass industry overview
India is strategically important to the ramming mass 
industry because of its expanding steel base, availability of 
silica resources and concentration of induction-furnace-led 
production clusters. Ken Research notes that the steel industry 
dominated India’s ramming mass market by application 
and links demand growth to continued expansion in steel 
production and infrastructure-led industrial activity.
A key structural development during the broader period was 
the Government of India’s decision to classify quartz as a 
major mineral effective February 2025, with implementation 
transition up to 30 June 2025. Since quartz is a critical 
raw material for silica-based ramming mass, this change 
has implications for regulation, supply formalization and 
resource governance.
The review of the Indian ramming mass industry is favourable 
because both demand-side and supply-side foundations 
improved. On the demand side, the strong performance 
of India’s steel industry supports recurring consumption of 
induction furnace lining materials; on the supply side, a more 
formal mineral classification framework can improve raw-
material discipline and market organization.
India also enjoys an ecosystem advantage in this segment 
because ramming mass demand is closely tied to steelmaking 
clusters where operational responsiveness matters. Suppliers 
that can ensure consistency, timely dispatch and technical 
reliability can create durable customer relationships in such 
concentrated industrial markets.
The Indian market therefore benefits from both growth and 
localization. As domestic steel production expands, locally 
Financial Statements
Statutory Reports
Company Overview
Annual Report 2025-26 | 21

===== PAGE 25 =====
available quartz-based inputs and a deeper manufacturing 
base can enhance cost competitiveness and reduce 
dependence on fragmented sourcing structures.
Overall, the review indicates an industry that is moving 
from being a supporting material segment to being a more 
organized industrial niche with stronger long-term relevance. 
This is particularly true in an economy where steelmaking 
capacity and induction furnace use continue to expand.
   Outlook
The outlook for India’s ramming mass industry remains 
favourable because its end-use base is expanding and its 
raw-material framework is becoming more formalized. The 
industry is expected to continue growing on the back of 
steel production expansion and policy support tied to India’s 
broader steel capacity ambitions.
The outlook is particularly constructive because India 
combines demand visibility with industrial scale. When a 
large and growing steel market sits alongside improving raw-
material governance, ancillary furnace-material industries 
usually gain both volume potential and better operating 
structure.
With more organized mineral oversight and sustained 
induction furnace usage, the industry appears positioned 
for scalable and more quality-conscious growth. This should 
especially benefit players that combine raw-material access, 
product consistency and strong relationships with steel and 
foundry customers.
The Indian ramming mass outlook therefore appears stronger 
than a simple commodity-supply story. It increasingly 
resembles an industrial solutions market where quality, 
repeat performance and integration with steel customers 
can become meaningful differentiators.
Factors driving ramming mass growth in India
The most important driver of ramming mass demand in India 
is the expansion of steel production and steel consumption. 
India produced 168.4 million tonnes of crude steel and 
consumed 163.7 million tonnes of finished steel in FY 2025-
26, while sector researchers identify steel as the dominant 
application segment for ramming mass in India.
A second key driver is the expected continuation of strong 
steel demand growth. The World Steel Association’s 
projection of about 9% growth in Indian steel demand in 
both 2025 and 2026 implies sustained furnace utilization 
and, by extension, recurring demand for lining materials 
such as silica ramming mass.
A third driver is the increased emphasis on efficiency and 
performance in melting operations. Global market research 
indicates that users are seeking critical consumables with 
stronger thermal resistance, better durability and energy-
efficiency benefits, which supports value-added growth in 
the ramming mass segment rather than purely volume-led 
growth.
A broader review of these drivers shows that ramming mass 
demand in India is not dependent on a single temporary 
catalyst. It is being supported by a chain of interlinked 
factors, namely steel capacity expansion, healthy domestic 
steel consumption, induction furnace usage, material-
performance 
expectations 
and 
better 
raw-material 
governance.
This combination is important because it improves the 
quality of growth. Demand arising from repeat furnace 
operations, productivity requirements and organized supply 
chains is generally more sustainable than demand driven 
only by one-time inventory stocking.
In practical terms, the factors driving ramming mass growth 
in India point toward an industry with both recurring base 
demand and room for value addition. Manufacturers that 
align technical quality with supply reliability are likely to 
benefit most from this evolving demand pattern.

BUSINESS AND FINANCIAL OVERVIEW
Monolithisch India Limited, incorporated in 2018, is engaged 
in the manufacturing of critical consumable products 
serving secondary steel sector that require high-temperature 
resistant material for their steel melting induction furnace 
applications. The Company’s registered office is located 
at Plot No. 381, Village Utaraha, P.S. Neturia, Purulia, 
West Bengal, while its corporate office is located at Cosy 
Corner, Burdwan Compound, Lalpur, Ranchi G.P.O., Ranchi, 
Jharkhand – 834001. The Company operates in the critical 
consumable materials segment, where product quality, 
thermal performance, consistency and timely dispatch are 
critical to customer operations. Its business is positioned 
around supplying industrial process materials to customers 
across sectors linked to heat-intensive manufacturing and 
metal processing, thereby participating in an essential input 
category within the broader industrial value chain. The 
consolidated financial statements for FY 2025–26 have been 
prepared in accordance with the Accounting Standards (AS) 
prescribed under Section 133 of the Companies Act, 2013 
read with the Companies (Accounts) Rules, 2014, and other 
generally accepted accounting principles in India (Indian 
GAAP).

Key risk
Risk description
Mitigation strategy
Demand and steel-cycle 
risk
Since the Company’s products are 
directly linked to furnace operations, 
any slowdown in secondary steel 
demand or volatility in customer 
production can affect offtake patterns. 
Management maintains close engagement with 
customers, tracks production trends, deepens its business 
presence within existing accounts and aligns production 
planning with demand visibility rather than speculative 
volume assumptions.
Raw material price 
variability
Fluctuations in key input costs can 
pressure margins and affect pricing 
decisions.
The Company mitigates this through calibrated sourcing, 
process efficiencies, tighter raw-material management 
and balanced pricing actions aimed at protecting 
customer relationships while preserving profitability.
Capacity expansion 
execution risk
Brownfield and greenfield projects 
carry risks related to delays, cost 
overruns, vendor coordination, 
commissioning setbacks and 
underutilisation.
Expansion is being executed in a phased and modular 
manner, using brownfield learnings for better layout 
planning, equipment selection, vendor coordination and 
staged ramp-up linked to demand visibility.
Product consistency risk 
at higher scale
As volumes increase, maintaining 
uniform product quality becomes 
critical because any inconsistency can 
affect lining life, customer trust and 
repeat business.
The Company has invested in automation, process 
standardisation, advanced machinery, stronger quality 
controls, testing systems and data-led monitoring to 
reduce batch-to-batch variation and rejection levels.
Logistics and supply-
chain disruption risk
Road-based dispatch dependence 
and rising volumes increase exposure 
to transporter availability, delivery 
delays and freight inefficiencies.
Management mitigates this through proximity to key 
steel belts, stronger transporter relationships, inventory 
buffers, dispatch planning and freight optimisation across 
inbound and outbound routes and has access to supply 
through railway wagon in emergency.
Customer concentration 
within operating clusters
Even with a diversified base, 
concentration within specific steel 
regions may expose the business to 
regional slowdowns or operating 
disruptions.
The Company is widening its customer footprint across 
eastern, central and adjacent markets while growing 
share within multiple plants and sites rather than 
depending on a few isolated accounts.
Acquisition and 
integration risk
The integration of MIGPL and future 
group expansions may create risks 
in process alignment, customer 
retention, quality systems and 
operational synergy capture.
Management has focused on smooth integration, 
preserving customer continuity, aligning sourcing 
and production planning, and building a more unified 
operating platform across group entities.
Regulatory and listed-
company compliance 
risk
As a newly listed entity, the Company 
faces greater obligations around 
disclosures, governance, insider-
trading controls and shareholder 
communication.
The Company has reinforced Board oversight, secretarial 
capability, compliance processes, governance policies, 
investor communication systems and digital controls 
around sensitive information.
Working capital and 
liquidity risk
Rapid growth can strain receivables, 
inventory and liquidity if not 
monitored closely.
The Board regularly reviews working capital metrics, 
and the Company continues to follow demand-backed 
expansion, disciplined cost control and phased capital 
deployment to avoid operational strain.
RISKS AND CONCERN
The Company has laid down a well-defined risk management mechanism covering the risk mapping and trend 
analysis, risk exposure, potential impact and risk mitigation process. A detailed exercise is being carried out to 
identify, evaluate, manage and monitor business and non-business risks. The Audit Committee and the Board 
periodically review the risks and suggest steps to be taken to manage/mitigate the same through a properly 
defined framework.

Key risk
Risk description
Mitigation strategy
Competition and pricing 
pressure
Organised and unorganised 
competitors may pursue aggressive 
pricing or expansion in key markets.
The Company avoids competing only on price and 
instead reinforces its moat through quality consistency, 
technical collaboration, responsiveness, service reliability 
and location-driven cost advantage.
 
Environmental and 
safety compliance risk
Processing generates fugitive dust 
crystalline silica particulates, exposing 
the workforce to occupational health 
risks, this may include respiratory 
diseases such as asthma, COPD and  
silicosis.
The Company mitigates particulate and environmental 
risks through integrated engineering solutions, site-
wide suppression systems, and enforced personal safety 
protocols. Industrial-grade pulse-jet baghouse filters 
actively capture and extract airborne silica particles at 
emission points. To prevent fugitive dust from vehicular 
transit and personnel movement, high-pressure water 
mist cannons are deployed along internal thoroughfares 
and loading zones. Furthermore, entering plant zones 
strictly requires the use of certified high-filtration 
respiratory masks and hearing protection (earplugs) 
across all staff and visitors and scheduled occupational 
health surveillance.
Talent and organisational 
bandwidth risk
Rapid scaling can stretch plant 
teams, management bandwidth and 
governance systems.
The Company has added plant, sales, finance, compliance 
and technical resources while combining promoter 
experience with professional management depth and 
stronger institutional processes.

Employees are the valuable assets and the strength of an

who have contributed to the growth and progress of the 
Company. The employees are imparted training on site and

by each employee at all levels and records its appreciation 
for the co-operation extended, but for which the present 
growth would not have been possible.
Safety remains paramount, with statutory safety committees, 
regular training drills, and suggestion mechanisms driving 
zero major incidents. The established suggestion committee 
channels employee feedback into operational improvements, 
fostering ownership across shop floor to management. The 
company’s skill development focus addresses industry-
wide talent gaps in various parts of operations, with hiring 
of external specialists complemented by internal capability 
building.​
Industrial relations remain stable, supported by statutory

across manufacturing, technical, commercial and support 
functions, providing a foundation for its ongoing expansion 
and operational priorities.

Capacity journey
From focused capacity to a scalable platform. A major 
brownfield expansion, the integration of MIGPL and the 
upcoming greenfield facility together form the backbone 
of our capacity ramp‑up. Our expansion has been 
demand‑linked, aligned to a higher share of business and 
deeper integration with existing customers.
Integrated architecture
Our manufacturing philosophy is rooted in vertical integration and interconnected production flows. Unlike fragmented 
operations where each unit functions in isolation, our facilities are designed as unified ecosystems where raw material 
processing, blending, quality assurance and dispatch are woven into a single, seamless flow.
This design eliminates inter‑stage delays, reduces material handling loss and creates a production environment where 
every step feeds naturally into the next. The result is faster throughput, tighter control and a network of facilities that 
operates with the efficiency of a single organism rather than a collection of machines.

The Big Story of
Manufacturing Capacity & Process Integration
Overview
At Monolithisch India Limited, manufacturing excellence is 
more than a capability; it is the foundation of our ability to 
convert installed capacity into scalable, dependable value 
for all stakeholders. Manufacturing at Monolithisch is not 
about volume alone. It is about building systems that think, 
processes that self‑correct, and facilities that are designed 
from the first brick to perform at the highest standard and 
consistently, at scale. Over the last few years, our scale‑up 
has combined various expansion, strategic acquisition and 
a new greenfield project. We do not just replicate plants; we 
upgrade them. Each new facility inherits the accumulated 
intelligence of the last, making our manufacturing 
ecosystem smarter with every milestone.
Today, as we approach 5,76,000 MTPA of installed capacity, 
the story is not just one of numbers. It is the story of an 
organisation that has turned manufacturing into a strategic 
moat built on integrated design, process discipline and 
technology‑driven operations.

Intelligence built in
Predictive maintenance: 
IoT‑enabled sensors across key 
stages provide early‑warning 
signals, allowing us to address 
potential issues before they 
become disruptions protecting 
uptime and safeguarding process 
stability.
Intellectual capital
Manufacturing excellence at Monolithisch is built 
on intellectual capital developed through years of 
experience in demanding furnace environments. The 
Company combines process knowledge, application 
expertise and continuous learning through its Heat 
Excellence Program, translating technical capabilities into 
actionable customer insights. Adoption of Six Sigma and 
zero-defect methodologies has further strengthened 
process discipline, enabling measured, repeatable and 
scalable improvements. This cumulative knowledge 
continues to deepen with every plant commissioned and 
operating cycle completed.
Real-Time Logistics Intelligence: 
Delivering Right and On Time
To ensure seamless fulfillment and eliminate delivery friction 
for our furnace customers, Monolithisch has deployed 
a centralized, IoT-powered fleet tracking and corridor 
management system. Linking our dispatch operations 
directly to customer plant gates, this platform provides live 
telematics, real-time geofencing, route compliance tracking, 
and dynamic ETA forecasting across all transit corridors. By 
capturing automated exception alerts—ranging from route 
halts to turn-around detention times—our digital logistics 
desk proactively preempts transit bottlenecks. This enhanced 
operational visibility supports more reliable and timely 
deliveries while helping minimise transit-related disruptions 
and preserve product integrity.
Automation and operational control
At Monolithisch India Limited, automation and process 
control are embedded across facilities to ensure 
standardised execution, minimise variability and enhance 
operational efficiency. Automated batching, mixing and 
material handling enable consistent performance as 
volumes scale. Data-backed monitoring and production 
planning further support faster decision-making, improved 
forecasting and timely corrective action.

Built Right, Delivered Right and Valued Right
Built right
Monolithisch India Limited is evolving its portfolio through 
a family of higher‑performance furnace‑lining materials 
specifically engineered for demanding induction applications. 
The range now spans multiple proprietary grades of 
specialised ramming mass, each tuned to different furnace 
sizes, operating conditions and make, enabling customers to 
choose compositions that best match their individual process 
requirements.
The Company is increasingly emphasising advanced grades 
ramming mass developed through research, field feedback 
and performance analysis at customer plants. The direction 
is clearly towards solutions that support longer lining 
life, greater thermal efficiency and more stable furnace 
operations.
BG-77
A dependable general-purpose grade offering consistent performance 
under standard operating conditions.
SGB-777
A higher-performance grade designed for intensive production cycles, 
delivering enhanced density and hot strength.
The Big Story of
Overview
Monolithisch India Limited is steadily evolving from a single-
product ramming mass supplier into a more solutions-led 
partner, where product development, quality discipline and 
customer relevance reinforce one another in a cohesive 
operating model. The Company’s approach reflects a clear 
philosophy built right, delivered right and valued right 
anchoring performance across the value chain.
This progression is evident in the shift towards higher-
performance grades, stronger process-led consistency 
and deeper technical engagement with customers. At the 
same time, there is a conscious effort to expand beyond 
the core offering through adjacent products, positioning 
the business as a broader solutions provider rather than 
a standalone material supplier. Quality is not treated as 
an outcome, but as a controlled, repeatable process, 
while customer engagement moves beyond supply into 
collaboration and responsiveness.

Monolithisch India Limited

===== PAGE 38 =====
SLM-999
An advanced grade engineered for elevated temperatures and demanding 
operating conditions, with strong thermal-shock resistance and lining 
durability.
LG-86
A cost-effective solution for light to mid-duty applications, balancing lining 
performance and operational efficiency.
SLM-980
Our most cost effective grade developed for varied charge mixes and 
changing operating conditions, supporting stable heat and lining integrity.
SGB-LIMITED
Our premium grade, developed for critical applications where enhanced 
lining life, furnace stability and thermal efficiency are essential.

FY 25–26: A year of performance at the customer end
FY25–26 was not only a year of stronger financials for Monolithisch India Limited; it was also a period when customer‑side 
outcomes became more visible and measurable. Capacity expansion, process improvement and new product grades 
translated into better lining performance, higher furnace availability and more efficient steel production at client plants.
Across key accounts, the Company’s specialised grades and customised formulations reinforced its role as a technical partner 
rather than a commodity supplier. The following case studies illustrate how this translated into higher heats per lining, 
reduced downtime and more stable operations for customers in different regions and segments.
Steel producer in West bengal witnesses 
higher productivity through SGB‑Limited
Context
AIC Group in West bengal integrated 
Monolithisch’s SGB‑Limited series into its 
induction furnace operations, looking to 
improve campaign life and productivity without 
compromising on safety or stability.
Intervention
Monolithisch worked closely with the customer’s 
technical team to fine‑tune product chemistry 
and optimise the heat cycle for the plant’s 
specific operating conditions. The solution 
combined the premium SGB‑Limited grade with 
application‑level guidance on heat cycle and 
furnace handling.
Impact
	
Increase of 15–20% additional heats per 
lining.
	
Reduction in consumption.
This case demonstrates how a 
performance‑oriented product upgrade, backed 
by technical collaboration, can directly lift both 
operating efficiency and throughput for a large, 
quality‑conscious steel producer.
Raipur cluster customer – lower downtime 
through tailored formulation
Context
A customer in Raipur was experiencing frequent 
maintenance interruptions and high patching 
requirements, which were eroding furnace 
availability and raising maintenance effort.
Intervention
Monolithisch designed a tailored specialised 
ramming mass solution for the specific furnace 
and operating profile. The formulation focused 
on improving lining stability and resistance 
to localised stress conditions, while keeping 
application practices practical for the plant team.
Impact
	
Noticeable reduction in patching frequency.
	
Higher furnace availability and smoother 
operating continuity.
The case highlights how customised blends, 
developed in close coordination with plant 
teams, help customers convert a recurrent 
maintenance pain‑point into a more stable, 
predictable operating pattern.

===== PAGE 41 =====
Governance impact
Strong governance at Monolithisch India Limited is not merely a regulatory requirement; it is an operating philosophy that 
enables disciplined growth, builds stakeholder trust and strengthens long-term value creation. The Company’s transition into 
a listed entity during FY 2025–26 has further accelerated its shift towards a more structured, transparent and process-driven 
governance framework, ensuring that scale is supported by accountability at every level.
Listing-led governance transformation
FY2025-26 marked our first year as a listed company. 
on the NSE SME platform. The successful IPO, raising 
Rs. 82.02 Crores, did more than strengthen our balance 
sheet; it raised the bar for governance and disclosure. We 
recognise that investor trust is earned through consistent 
transparency and disciplined management, hence we by 
our own will opted for quarterly NSE reporting which is 
unseen in SME space and this transition has embedded 
stronger reporting practices, sharper financial discipline 
and enhanced stakeholder communication across the 
organisation.
Board oversight and strategic direction
	
The Board has been strengthened with a 
balanced mix of 3 executive, 1 non-executive 
and 2 independent directors, bringing diverse 
perspectives and deeper oversight to strategic 
decision-making. Board deliberations during the year 
were closely aligned with the Company’s growth 
priorities - capacity expansion, capital allocation, risk 
management and operational scalability.
	
The governance framework is further supported 
by a strengthened leadership layer, with the Chief 
Financial Officer driving financial discipline, capital 
efficiency and investor transparency, while the 
Company Secretary ensures robust compliance, 
regulatory alignment and effective Board processes.
	
The committee structure ensures focused 
governance across key areas such as audit, 
nomination and remuneration, and stakeholder 
relationships. Regular reviews and structured 
discussions enable the Board to guide long-term 
direction while maintaining strong control over 
execution risks.
38 | Annual Report 2025-26
Monolithisch India Limited

===== PAGE 42 =====
Risk management and transparency
	
As the business scales, risk management has evolved 
into a more structured and proactive framework. 
The Company has identified various risks and has 
integrated them into its decision-making processes.
	
Regular monitoring mechanisms, strengthened 
compliance systems and enhanced disclosures 
ensure that risks are not only mitigated but also 
communicated transparently to stakeholders. 
This approach builds confidence while enabling 
the Company to respond effectively to a dynamic 
operating environment.
Internal controls and compliance systems
	
Post listing, the Company has significantly 
strengthened its internal control environment. The 
finance and secretarial functions have been upgraded 
with experienced professionals, improving reporting 
quality, compliance rigour and financial discipline.
	
Robust systems have been implemented for regulatory 
compliance, including structured processes for 
disclosures, insider trading controls and governance 
oversight. The adoption of quarterly financial reporting 
further enhances transparency and provides consistent 
visibility to investors.
	
In addition, the appointment of joint auditors has 
strengthened audit quality and independence, 
reinforcing the credibility of financial reporting.
Ethics, accountability and stakeholder trust
	
The Company’s governance philosophy is anchored 
in ethical conduct, fairness and accountability. The 
Company has institutionalised policies such as Code 
of Conduct, insider trading regulations and related-
party transaction frameworks to ensure that operations 
remain transparent and compliant.
	
Internal monitoring mechanisms have been 
strengthened to ensure that all related-party 
transactions are conducted at arm’s length and in the 
ordinary course of business. This disciplined approach 
reinforces credibility and safeguards stakeholder 
interests.
	
The Stakeholders’ Relationship Committee actively 
oversees investor communication and grievance 
resolution, ensuring timely response and transparent 
engagement with shareholders.

4.	 Pursuant to the provisions of Section 105 of the 
Act, a Member entitled to attend ad vote at the 
AGM is entitled to appoint a Proxy to attend and 
vote on his/ her behalf and the Proxy need not 
be a Member of the Company. However, since 
this AGM is being held pursuant to the applicable 
MCA Circulars as mentioned hereinabove, through 
VC / OAVM, physical attendance of Members has 
been dispensed with. Accordingly, the facility for 
appointment of Proxies by the Members will not 
be available for the AGM and hence the Proxy 
Form and Attendance Slip are not annexed to this.

the route map of the venue of the Meeting is not 
annexed hereto.

through VC/ OAVM are requested to send to

Resolution together with attested specimen 
signature of the duly authorized signatory(ies) who 
are authorized to attend and vote at the Meeting 
on their behalf.

Item No. 4: To approve the appointment of M/s. R K Jagetiya & Co., Chartered Accountants (Firm Registration 
No. 146264W), Mumbai, as Joint Statutory Auditors of the Company for a term of three (3) consecutive 
years:
The Board of Directors of the Company, upon the recommendation of the Audit Committee, has proposed the 
appointment of M/s. R K Jagetiya & Co., Chartered Accountants (Firm Registration No. 146264W), Mumbai, as Joint 
Statutory Auditors of the Company, in addition to the existing Statutory Auditors, M/s. P N & Company, Chartered 
Accountants (Firm Registration No. 016783C).
The proposed appointment is intended to further strengthen the Company’s audit and assurance framework, 
enhance financial oversight, improve governance standards and support the Company’s growing business 
operations and regulatory compliance requirements. Considering the increasing scale and complexity of the 
Company’s operations and keeping in view the enhanced compliance requirements applicable to listed companies, 
the Board believes that the appointment of Joint Statutory Auditors would be in the best interests of the Company 
and its stakeholders.
M/s. R K Jagetiya & Co. have conveyed their consent to act as Joint Statutory Auditors of the Company and have 
confirmed that their appointment, if made, would be within the limits prescribed under the Companies Act, 2013 
and that they satisfy the criteria provided under Sections 139 and 141 of the Companies Act, 2013. The Company 
has also received a certificate from the said firm confirming their eligibility for appointment as Joint Statutory 
Auditors.
The Audit Committee and the Board of Directors have reviewed the credentials, experience, expertise and peer 
review status of M/s. R K Jagetiya & Co. and are satisfied that the firm possesses the requisite knowledge, capability 
and experience to effectively discharge the responsibilities of Joint Statutory Auditors of the Company.
Accordingly, the Board of Directors recommends the Ordinary Resolution set out at Item No. 4 of the Notice for 
approval of the Members.
None of the Directors, Key Managerial Personnel of the Company or their relatives is concerned or interested, 
financially or otherwise, in the proposed Resolution except to the extent of their shareholding, if any, in the 
Company.
Item No. 5: Ratification of Remuneration of Cost Auditor payable for the Financial Year 2026-27:
The Board of Directors of the Company, on the recommendation of the Audit Committee, has approved the 
appointment of Mr. Ajay Deep Wadhwa (CMA), Proprietor of M/s Ajay Deep Wadhwa & Associates, Practicing Cost 
and Management Accountants, as the Cost Auditor of the Company for conducting the audit of the cost records 
maintained by the Company for the financial year ending March 31, 2027.
In accordance with the provisions of Section 148 of the Companies Act, 2013 read with the Companies (Cost 
Records and Audit) Rules, 2014, the remuneration payable to the Cost Auditor is required to be ratified by the 
Members of the Company.
Accordingly, the Board has approved a remuneration of Rs. 30,000/- (Rupees Thirty Thousand Only) per annum, 
inclusive of applicable taxes and out-of-pocket expenses, if any, payable to M/s. Ajay Deep Wadhwa & Associates 
for conducting the cost audit for the financial year 2026-27, subject to ratification by the Members.
The Board recommends the Ordinary Resolution set out at Item No. 5 of the Notice for approval of the Members.
